ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-15-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00162_d8e8291	Mr President! Ladies and gentlemen! Values Advisors online and also here in the hall! First of all, a warm excuse for my short delay. We are dealing with the Gas Economic Act today. Again!, some may now be trying to say. We have been attacking the law in recent months as often as we have in recent years, decades ago, but it is a matter of adapting to a new strategic situation, to equip us well for the coming winter. Mrs Doppelbauer has referred to it as: We face challenges that we could hardly have imagined a few years ago, months ago, because energy supply, which we have built on for years and decades, is all of a sudden no longer self-evident because Russia uses energy supplies as a weapon, because Putin puts us under pressure with prices. We feel very clearly – and this has already come out more often in the debate – how vulnerable we are, how dependent our economy is. We all have in mind how many apartments are heated with natural gas. Even if it will take years – and this is a bitter truth, we must look into it – to free ourselves from this dependence, we must start now: No walk, no act of strength, I have already said that at this point, and this act of strength will still require us all to do so. At the same time – I am not going to repeat everything now – we have already put in place measures in recent months to ensure that the supply is ensured so that the resilience of our economy increases. The central buffer that we have – and Mr Graf has already pointed out – is the filling of memory, so all the strength in our work is first on the question: How do we get full memory? because this is our buffer in the crisis case. Today, we are also discussing two proposals which are precisely aimed at freeing ourselves from dependence step by step and thus making us more resilient. The first is the novel to the Gas Economics Act, to the GWG 2011. These are three main concerns: First of all, it is about the goal – quite clearly – to connect all natural gas storage to the Austrian network and to set us up for security of supply in Austria in such a way that all natural gas storage can contribute to Austria. Therefore, in the GWG-Novelle, there is a proposal for an obligation to connect all storage facilities in the Austrian territory to the market area. Storage operators have to take all structural measures for a network connection, especially for connecting to the distribution network – this is faster. As soon as the physical network connection is established, storage companies will also have to apply for network access and network access. This, of course, concerns the storage system, which has often been the subject of today's debate, namely Haidach, which has only been able to store over the German market area in Austria. The second measure, which has already been mentioned, also concerns the storage of Haidach. As you know and as mentioned today, a part of the storage – not the whole but part of the storage – is marketed by a subsidiary of the Russian Gazprom. It is currently massively stored in all stores, partly at the technical maximum, which is good. In Haidach we don't see any activities – today's stand. We are currently equipped with over 37 terawatt hours of natural gas in our storage, but a storage is empty: The Gazprom part in the Haidach storage is empty, and we simply can't afford that in this situation. We all need available storage to provide the best possible equipment for the winter. That is why the proposal in the GWG-Novelle is exactly the same: Storage users who do not systematically benefit from their capacities must offer them on a secondary platform or return them to the physical storage company. So simple: If you don't use the memory, you should make it available to others. [[Congratulation with the Greens and the Abg. Weidinger.]] What happens if the memory user fails to comply with this obligation? – Then the storage company withdraws from the storage user exactly these systematically unused storage capacities: Use it or lose it, often already used in this context as a name for this scheme. With the Use it or lose it regulation, we intend to use the most limited means, but we have a goal to achieve effectively, namely that the storage of Haidach is filled. This is the goal and the top priority. The third measure in this GWG-Novelle is an authorisation for me to conclude a Convention on the common use of storage equipment. In particular, this concerns: At European level, we have decided on the obligation to fill the storage to 80 percent. That is what we have decided in Austria as the Federal Government. This filling also concerns the storage of Haidach – see the measures mentioned above – whose filling is, in particular, a common responsibility of Germany and Austria at European level. We can only deal with this Union-law fulfilment goal in an agreement with Germany, and that is why it is precisely in this novel to authorize me to make a convention with Germany for this purpose. At the technical level, this is already being worked on, which is a very constructive dialogue that we are conducting with Germany, on all the topics, including the topics that Mrs Double Bauer mentioned earlier, because this is important: We're going to have Europe is in this situation together, and we are in this situation in solidarity. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] I would like to refer briefly to the Gas Diversification Act, because I said: Full memory is an insurance for the next winter. – Yes, well, important, but there is no doubt at all that there is real independence and thus real security only if we are not so dependent. That is why we need to reduce the dependence on Russian gas and finally end it by gradually reducing the imports of natural gas. This includes less gas consumption, for example by changing gas heating to renewable alternatives. The renewable heat law has already fallen. I am really very, very glad that we can now go into consideration with this law, after more than 100 working group meetings with the federal states, dear Alois Schroll, three political governing bodies with the national councils, after two decisions in the national conference – a really well-prepared, intensively debated law that does, above all, one, namely to pack the evil at the root, to reduce the dependence on gas in an area where we have a quarter of our gas consumption, namely in the heat sector. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Of course, this includes other suppliers, and precisely because our dependence on Russian natural gas is so large, this area is particularly difficult for Austria. As you know, the pipeline infrastructure is historically oriented towards Russia. Austria was the first western country to be connected to the Russian gas grid. We are an interior and do not have seaports for their own import. Nevertheless, we have no time to lose. We took first steps: With the increase of the strategic gas reserve with non-Russian gas – this call for tender will start soon – we will press the dependence from 80 to 70 percent this year. There are several companies in which gas flows from non-Russian sources. But that's not enough. We need further steps, and that is why we want to take the necessary measures by means of the Gas Diversification Act. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] From this year to 2025, 100 million euros are available each year so that companies can receive additional costs for the import of non-Russian natural gas. This can be about increased costs for delivery to Austria – management rights, longer routes – or additional costs for companies for the use of natural gas from non-Russian sources. We deliberately support this as well as in Germany; in Germany, the costs are transferred to the network charges, but thus they are also directly charged to the end customers. We did not want to make this known in Austria, which is why the proposal you are here today to promote directly, so that it is not passed on to the final customers. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Finally, as a second measure under this law, we want to support those companies that are currently operating exclusively with gas to the extent that they can also be operated alternatively to natural gas with other energy sources. This is an important measure in terms of crisis resistance. However, I am convinced that this is also an important step towards greater independence and will help us to significantly reduce the share of our gas imports from Russia. Summary: The challenge we face is undisputed. It's good to keep this in mind again and again. This effort is far from over. The first measures we have taken are already effective. That's good. The gas stores fill quickly. Today, more than one third of our annual consumption is in the Austrian stores. In the European comparison, we are the second in terms of the quantity we have stored in relation to the share of our annual consumption: more than one third of our annual consumption. Today's GWG-Novelle provides the basis for connecting Haidach's storage memory to allow us to benefit from storage capacities that are not systematically used, in the sense of the security of our country's supply as a critical infrastructure. We promote the diversification of gas supply. All these are important steps on the way to being independent of Russian natural gas by 2027. This is a good and important goal for our Republic, so I would like to thank you for your support.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-15-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00162_d8e10370	Madam President. Dear People's Lawyers! Ladies and gentlemen in the House! The People's Procuratorate has developed over the years into an indispensable element of control. That is why I should like to congratulate you three times today: First to 45 years of popular prosecution, 45 years for the people. Since 1977, the People's Procuratorate has been there for all people, regardless of age, gender, place of residence and nationality. The beginnings of the People's Procuratorate were modest. At first, it was a temporary commission with 18 planning points, which has been extended to 92 planning points by the tasks that have been carried out. I should also like to congratulate a truly successful celebration, in which a speaker said very appropriately: If the People's Procuratorate hadn't invented yet, you'd have to invent her! [[Congratulation with the ÖVP and the Abg. Koza.]] In 2021, some 23,600 people responded with their complaints to the public prosecutor’s office – 32 percent more than in the year 2020 – and 11 516 cases started examination proceedings. The results of the examination are now available in the report. I would just like to take one example so that you can see how efficiently the People's Procuratorate works: A young man turned to the People's Procuratorate because, because of his transsexuality, he was automatically declared unsuitable at the Federal Army's post commission. The public prosecutorship has helped him in agreement with the ministry, and now the young man is happy to do his service. Secondly, I should like to congratulate Opcat-Mandat for ten years – that is the implementation of the Optional Protocol to the UN Convention on Torture and other cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment with which the People's Procuratorate ==References== ==References== This is about protecting against violations of human rights. For this, there are seven Commissions who also worked in 2021. There were 570 missions in nursing homes, secondary centres, judicial institutions, and preventive good work was done. However, I can also congratulate a third thing, and since 2009 the secretariat of the IOI has been located in Austria. The People's Procuratorate is also active in the sense of human rights. The IOI is an international association that has received the legal status of an international body based on the Act of Headquarters. In addition, the People's Procuratorate was granted the A status of the United Nations. I would like to congratulate and thank you very much for this, as well as for the international work. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I would also like to give an example of a case where, as a Parliament, we are immediately implementing the public prosecutors' suggestions: The latest Public Prosecutor's Report on Human Rights Control shows that there is no meaningful data in statistics on people with disabilities in particular. However, these are often important in order to improve the basis for their living situation. For this reason, the government parties today also want to put in place a resolution to this effect. So we act immediately and we do things the same way. In this sense, I would like to express my warm thanks to all three lawyers and to all their employees, because they do great things. ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-15-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00162_d8e10737	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen. Ladies and gentlemen! I must first apologize to our current chairman Walter Rosenkranz. There is no more important date than the discussion with Parliament, but we have very important cooperations, among others with the ORF and the mission “Bürgeranwalt”. This is a very, very important means for the People's Procuratorate to approach the people and promote low level of access to the People's Procuratorate. That's why we split up today: Mr Rosenkranz has the dates at ORF, we are speaking and replying to the House. I can say, in his representation, a few general things about the reports that are being discussed today. It has already been mentioned, the incitement to the People's Procuratorate has increased. We have had more than 23 000 complaints, and, Mr Blimlinger, we are not afraid of people. If more comes, they will also find open doors and an open ear. We are pleased if we can support people who have problems with the Austrian administration or who demand that we control institutions in which potential evasions of freedom, human rights violations could take place. This year, however, I am particularly pleased to give the praise that some speakers have expressed to our employees, because they have to deal with this increased attack and do so with Bravour. Just so you can imagine: Of these 23 000 complaints that we received from us in 2021, more than half lead to an elaborate test procedure. That is to say, there is a rather intense correspondence with authorities, rather intense legal assessments, attempts to solve the problem between the citizen, the citizen and the authority within the framework of the laws, and very often solutions to be satisfactory. The reverse conclusion that the other 11 000, 12 000 complaints that come to us are therefore ignored is not allowed. We also deal with complaints where we do not immediately initiate an investigation procedure or come into contact with authorities, of course we are dealing with, and every person who turns to the public prosecutors receives an answer – hopefully, if we cannot help directly, because it is not a problem with the administration, even with a corresponding tip to whom you can contact. In addition to the auditing of the administration, we have carried out 570 visits during our inspections in the framework of preventive human rights control. That is, while in 2020 the pandemic still has a bit of a disability, in 2021 we carried out the visits to the fullest extent, as in the years before. We were there personally, we took all precautions. As far as I am concerned know, no cluster has been created by a visit to the public prosecutor's office. On the other hand, some people who work in the commissions have become infected, so we had to act very flexibly, but this has generally worked well. Now to my business, I would like to highlight four things from my business unit: One is corona management. It has already been said that the rise in complaints is due not only to Corona, but of course also. It is a new situation, we have written an extra report on this situation in 2020, hoping that this special report will help to respond to things better. This hope, unfortunately I must say, has only partially fulfilled itself. Even in 2021, Corona management was not optimal. This started at the beginning of the year with a relative chaos in vaccination, prioritizing who comes first. There have been a lack of federal standards. High-risk groups have been preferred in some federal states and have not been favoured in other federal states, which has led to great displeasure. The complaints about it were numerous. Of course, we could see that high-risk groups are being preferred, but the bitterness about the differences between the federal states was great in the population. Unfortunately, a mistake that was made in 2020 has repeated in 2021: There have been very short-term changes in the legal situation. In general, this is always a problem in the administration if the authorities hear a policy announcement that they are about to get the text of the regulation only at the last second and then in the week after, in the extreme case, even the day after, to apply a regulation which they did not get to face before. This, of course, leads to uncertainty in the authority, which leads to uncertainty among the standard subjects, which leads to wrong decisions, and unfortunately it was also 2021 to be observed. There have been problems with the issue of recovery certificates, with the collection in the green pass. In some cases, a third vaccination, which was recommended, was indicated in the passport only as a second vaccination, which, of course, has also led to problems. We have also been able to solve this problem. Problems with contact tracking, with separate modes there are still and there were then. These have, of course, very unpleasant effects for individuals – remember that for people who have been in quarantine, the salary refunding depends on the secretion notice. The second problem I would like to address is a problem with the Ministry of Family Affairs concerning the payment of child allowance and childcare allowance. While this was a case with the child allowance, which occurred rather singularly in 2021 due to the simultaneous changeover to the Austrian Financial Office and a retrofit resulting from non-certifications in the Corona era, the problem with child care money is unfortunately a permanent one. We have, in order to make it short, fifty cases in which parents wait for years for the childcare allowance, partly until the children come to school. That's not the point of child care money, it's definitely fixed. In the prevention of human rights control, I would like to point out that what has already been said by speakers in the debate is very, very serious. There is a direct link between the lack of staff in different institutions and the quality of care and the frequency of human rights violations or threats. This applies not only to old-age and care homes – there are the examples currently particularly tragic – but of course also to disabled facilities in the same way. How to provide youth assistance facilities, the Psychiatrics, but also hospitals. There is absolutely a need to counter it, there you have to think about it, because too few staff lead to people being detained, medicated, ill-treated – and this must not happen in a modern social state. [[In the case of the SPÖ, representatives of the ÖVP and the Agg. Krisper.]] My last point concerns the home-sacrifice pension, the treatment of the injustice of the past: Again, we had 350 cases in which people reported themselves to us, who had been housed in a children's home or a nursing family between 1945 and 1999 and who had experienced violence there. We have found a law gap that I do not want to keep from you: In itself, people who are no longer working and are no longer able to work should receive a pension or minimum pension due to this no longer existing work capacity, receive this home-sacrifice pension if they have been accommodated and have experienced violence. We have discovered several cases in which this is not the case. If the person concerned lives in a partnership and the partner, who deserves so much that you do not get a minimum pension, and has no pension claim of his own, you do not get the pension either. I'm sure that's not the point. I would ask you to look at it and take the necessary legal measures. Thank you very much.

In the last five years, there has been a 60 percent increase in the allocations to the implementation of the measures. This does not, of course, correspond to the reality of life of individuals who can work around the globe, that only the fact that you were not working for a full year in Austria leads to a poor situation when it comes to being able to work on the Austrian labour market. This issue has been addressed in a moderate and factual manner by the Public Prosecutor's Office to the competent ministry, which in this case, of course, has contacted the AMS immediately, and I can They mentioned here that not only the parallel process at the Administrative Court has come to the same conclusion as the People's Procuratorate suggested, but also that the competent ministry's adoption is on the way to an adjustment in order to enable qualified skilled workers in shortage professions to access the Austrian labour market more quickly. I think that is a valuable, important achievement. That is why I would like to conclude by stressing my thanks again: With your work, you contribute to the fact that needs, concerns and problems are addressed to the Hohe Haus, the legislative body, and we can then implement solutions together with you for the benefit of the Austrians. Last sentence from me – and I would like to take up the thought of the valued colleague Eva Blimlinger, who finally said this: Please, dear Austrians, dear Austrians and all the people who live in this country, if you have a topic, if you have distress and distress, please also contact the People's Procuratorate! This service is free, but not for free.

(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) But also appreciated ladies and gentlemen at home in front of the screens and also here at the gallery! At this item on the agenda, we are now discussing the report of the People's Procuratorate for the last year 2021. With the People's Procuratorate we have a very special institution. The Public Prosecutorship provides an important assistance function for those citizens who feel inadequate or unfairly treated by the administration on the one hand. Around 23,600 people turned to the People's Procuratorate in 2021. This corresponds to an increase of say and write 32 percent compared to 2020. The pandemic will certainly also have contributed to this. Over 11 500 test procedures were then finally initiated. As an independent control body, the public prosecutor has the task of examining and clarifying alleged or suspected maladministrations in the administration. However, it often helps to clarify misunderstandings very easily and to provide a solution in this way. The report now before us, however, not only provides evidence of maladministration, but it is very often possible to read out some positive and then use it on. Reading the report of the Public Procuratorate will soon also be noted: There are problems that affect a few people, but there are also problems that affect many people. The spectrum is therefore a very broad one. I would like to take a positive example. In the years 2018 and 2019, Austria, which is well known, has suffered extensive forest damage caused by snow, storm, as well as borque beetles. The National Council decided on 7 July to abolish the losses which had occurred at the time and related them to the farmers and the large forestry farms. In July 2020 the Forest Fund was set up by the Forest Fund Act. With the Forest Fund, a large package for our forests has been interconnected: 350 million euros for ten measures, all of which we, too, sit here, ultimately benefit. It is well known: The forest protects against natural hazards, it stores CO2, it produces vital oxygen, it filters the water we all appreciate in our lives. It is habitat for numerous animals and plants, provides people with the necessary relaxation room and provides us with the sustainable raw material wood. The establishment of the forest fund was thus a success model. The Public Prosecutor's Office contributed to the implementation of this Forest Fund's proposals for solutions from the practical problems raised to them. That's the way it should be, because you have to develop. In this sense: Thank you very much for the work the public prosecutor has done on many dates online or on site! Every solved problem, whether small or large, makes our home even more beautiful and life-worthy. Thank you. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] 13.21

ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-15-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00162_d8e12424	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! We are debating here a novel of the Federal Ministry Act, which is related to the resignations of the ministers Köstinger and Schramböck. The centrepiece is the aggregation of the Ministry of Labour and Economic Affairs, and it must be noted that such shifts of competence are not necessarily necessary, but – on the contrary – are always made only at the beginning of a legislative period, because such a shift is linked to a high organisational, bureaucratic and financial effort. It is the question of whether the civil service in the two ministries should deal with things such as changing the logos, the door signs, the business cards, the creation of new e-mail addresses, the stamping of envelopes, letter paper and, above all, the aggregation, consolidation of IT. – That sounds like little things now, but it's not. This leads to many night shifts, the officials are rotating now, that costs summaries. The question arises whether the officials, who already have to cope with the deficits of this federal government and the many resignations, need to be overloaded with such things now. I think that is very difficult. But, well, the division of competence is one thing and the aggregation of the Ministry of Labour and Economic Affairs is something else. Whether the organisational is meaningful or not – is crucial, I think, the content orientation. I am not quite sure whether the government of this foreign currency by Franz Josef Strauss, politics is being made with the head, which Mr Gerstl has quoted here, really always follows, because the new superminister Kocher of course now comes to another huge task: “It’s the economy” – that’s also what President Biden is currently working hard in the USA. Yes, it is the economy that brings us freedom, security and prosperity when you leave it. The ÖVP always sings the high song of the free market economy, but has not taken this into account at all in the last two years when one considers the overwhelming corona measures and, unfortunately, the most irrational sanctions and climate policy. If we look at what is going on now, we should compare this with the situation in Switzerland, which is somehow in a similar situation to Austria. I recommend to the new Minister Kocher, who now also takes over the Ministry of Economic Affairs, a look into Switzerland before he starts, because the external circumstances, which are always held responsible by the government for our current difficult situation – on the one hand, Corona, on the other hand, the war – apply equally to Switzerland. Now, in the last two years, Switzerland has pursued a much more moderate, more important corona policy, much shorter lockdowns, closures, school closures, starting and contact restrictions. It also always kept an eye on the economic impact with all the consequences from the beginning and is now also carrying out a real evaluation. To the war: This is not the case with Ukraine, but also with the Swiss media, with all the economic consequences, on the one hand, on Russia, and on the other hand, on those who impose sanctions, as in this case Switzerland. Just briefly on the example of oil embargo: Switzerland has now joined – it has said that the EU has pushed this way – but is hardly affected itself because it receives only 0.3 percent oil from Russia. From the very beginning, however, she pointed out that the oil embargo is nonsense, because the debate on it alone will drive the price very strongly. That's exactly what happened and that doesn't happen to Russia – quite the contrary: Russia benefits from these higher prices – but the EU is hit by it, and it hits us in the market. That's what it looks like now. In other words, in the last two years, Switzerland has had fewer lockdowns, fewer dead, less lignite, less inflation – this is now 2.8/2.9 percent – and also a hard currency like the Swiss franc. There's a lot of things we could look away from. What a Swiss professor who teaches in Linz also said: The Austrians finally have to take care that the economy needs a perspective, need confidence. If you read the news again today: Double as many new infections as last week. - That won't bring us on. We must be optimistic despite everything, because only in this way can the economy function and only in this way can we preserve prosperity, security and freedom. Thank you.

This innovation regulates technical training, but also the recognition of acquired rights and, of course, also the duties of the Chamber of Dentists. Technical training should take place in a university, at least three years, and there should be a theoretical part, but also a practical part. It is particularly ideally solved that the practical part can also be completed in recognised orthopaedic teaching practices or teaching ambulatorys. The rights acquired are also taken into account, for the professional name can also be governed by those with acquired rights. With this law we meet the European law requirements for the specialist in pine orthodontics, so it is also possible that this training is recognised throughout Europe. We have created training at an international level. In this sense, I am pleased that we are taking a unanimous decision here today. Thank you.

In this way, the Ministry of Social Affairs is following a resolution in Parliament from December 2021. Why is this standardisation so important for people with disabilities? – Accessibility is one of the general principles set out in Article 3 of the UN Convention on the Rights of the Disabled and is a prerequisite for people with disabilities to live self-determined, to share fully and equally in society and to exercise their rights. Following the basic idea of the Designed for All, products should in principle be usable for all users without any additional adjustments. Accessibility and Universal Design are therefore also included in the UN Disability Convention as essential conditions for the inclusion of people with disabilities. Almost all the things we consume are subject to standards. These are guidelines for the design of products and services. In the economy, standards are used to offer products and services of standardised quality. In standardisation bodies, very large industrial companies or groups are inside to represent their interests in products and services, but civil society organisations and consumer interest representatives, including people with disabilities, are rarely taken into account or have too quiet a voice. This leads to an imbalance, and the interests are too little taken into account. Especially in the case of people with disabilities, this leads to the fact that we have already heard correctly several times that they cannot participate. If you change something afterwards, this is an extreme effort, such as for programmers of websites that are not barrier-free. This is not all as fast as you think from the outset that this has to be barrier-free, and is very cost-intensive. So what are we planning to do with this new law that Minister Rauch has already spoken about? – If representatives of a wide range of consumers are not represented or only few are represented in standardisation bodies – I have already mentioned that – then the needs are not taken sufficiently into account. That is why this own specialist body is to be set up for standardisation. This should focus, among other things, on people with disabilities and accessibility. This means, in concrete terms, that the introduction of the interests of people with disabilities in which standardisation is ensured. There is cooperation with the Austrian Disability Council, the maintenance of contacts with organisations whose objective includes consumer protection or accessibility. Col. Pfurtscheller has already explained what a banknote means when you sit in the wheelchair. You can also try this if you are not sitting in the wheelchair. Check it out in front of a banknote: You will not see what you enter or type in. If the sun shines, this is totally impossible. This is about the perspective of a wheelchair driver in front of a banknote. Among other things, this will be taken into account now: Banknotes, banking services, streaming services, smartphones – the cables have already been addressed – e-book readers. But it is also about the situation if you want to buy a ticket for a concert, i.e. online services. For the future and for us Greens, it is also important that people with disabilities can use e-cars. These are charged at e-charge stations. Take a look at how e-charge stations are still being installed! These are often on a podcast. For example, when you sit in the wheelchair, you come because the height of the podst on which this tank column is on it is so high, not with the arm – that far it is not enough – to the socket. Point report of the Committee on Human Rights on amendment 2601/A(E) by Mr Dr. Ewa Ernst-Dziedzic, Dr. Gudrun Kugler, colleagues in respect of respect for human rights and international humanitarian law in Ukraine and individual criminal responsibility (1512 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-15-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00162_d8e28006	We're coming to the 18th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mrs Kugler is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). The word stands with her. - Here you go.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-15-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00162_d8e28034	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! We are now discussing the issue of respect for human rights and international humanitarian law in Ukraine and, most importantly, individual criminal responsibility. In our request, we have talked about Austria contributing to the work being done by the International Criminal Court in Ukraine. The International Criminal Court – perhaps not all of them yet know so well – goes back to the Roman Statute – was founded in 2000 to 2002. In English it is known under ICC, in German under IStGH, International Criminal Court. This ICC constitutes a revolution in international law, for it was not possible for international law to be directly applied to an individual in a state until the Roman Statute. The Roman Statute has made this possible for the most serious crimes of international importance, i.e. crimes against humanity, war crimes and genocide. Now neither Russia nor Ukraine are parties to the International Criminal Court. 124 countries are, but they are not. However, in 2013 and 2014, Ukraine recognised the jurisdiction of the Court of Justice by way of a declaration, thus covering the territory of Ukraine. Even if Russia itself is not present, then the jurisdiction of the International Criminal Court applies to anyone – whoever – who commits war crimes in that country. It may be interesting to say that the International Criminal Court can also condemn someone if this person has acted in accordance with their legal order and at the command of their superiors. The question, of course, arises: How can you justify that? – I believe that international criminal law has actually found a very exciting answer. It says: Despite its own legal position and the order of the Supreme One, this person should have known that war crimes must not be committed. But the question is, if someone is to be condemned, whether the evidence is then secured, whether one can prove that what is being accused has actually happened. This is the big question in Ukraine: Will evidence be destroyed? Can they be secured? – This is something that can help a neutral country like Austria. We already have 100 000 euros at the International Criminal Court provided and we help with personnel and expertise so that in the case of war crimes that have already occurred in Ukraine and which are happening now, evidence can be secured, witnesses can be heard. We have already read a lot about this in the media in recent days. The use of Streumunition was documented by Amnesty International a few days ago – this is prohibited by international law. The question is how the prisoners of war are going. The Red Cross is not allowed to go there, it does not know how people are doing. The issue of sexual violence, sexual violence as a weapon against women and children and as an element of strategic warfare: This too is prohibited by international law. But there are very many statements that these things happen, unfortunately also to a very, very large extent. A person concerned has used a word that is very significant. She speaks of intergenerational trauma, a trauma that is passed on from generation to generation, and she says that it is the intention to destroy generations – not just one but several – to destroy a country. It is possible to rebuild infrastructure, but what is happening to these people cannot simply be rekindled. That is why it is important that one also has a woman-specific access, in the way we try to help humanitarianly. I am pleased that there is a request in the Committee on Equal Opportunities, which will be very, very well summarized and discussed there next week. Austria supports the work of the International Criminal Court. There are already 40 experts working in Ukraine. We'll carry that. I would like to invite all of you to agree to this request, which would like to stress this once again and also to strengthen it. I do not want to end here without highlighting further commitment to Ukraine, and that is the work done by the OSCE. The OSCE has really also been founded with the concern for peace in Europe, namely the OSCE as an institution, but also the Parliamentary Assembly of the OSCE – I am particularly pleased that our colleague Reinhold Lopatka is working as a Special Representative. Perhaps by means of these many parts, in which all countries are involved at different levels and in different institutions, one can come to a rapid end of the war and war crimes. Thank you.

Jörg Leichtfried, colleagues in a federal law amending the Federal Constitutional Act and the Federal Law on the Rules of Procedure of the National Council (Reg. 1975 Act) (2509/A)
ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-15-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00162_d8e29529	We come to Agenda item 20. We are going into the debate. Mr Ottenschlager is speaking on behalf of the PPE Group. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-15-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00162_d8e29556	Mr President. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) First reading: Request by the SPÖ for an amendment of the Federal Constitutional Act and the Rules of Procedure of the National Council. What are the contents? – The President of the Court of Auditors or the President of the Council will be elected by two-thirds in the future; [[Unrest in the hall]] increase the number of requests made by the National Council for special audits by the Court of Auditors; Publication by the Court of Auditors of studies, opinions and surveys by ministries; the Court of Auditors is to examine the ministries every two years with a view to supporting parties through substantial contributions. The request, of course, is in a direct, close relationship with the proposal for an initiative of the strengthening of the party law which we have tabled. I believe that this is also in agreement with the Social Democratic Party, and I think we will continue to negotiate and discuss it. What are the essential points of the hopefully soon to be decided law? – strengthening the audit by the Court of Auditors, coupled with the Court of Auditors' direct right of access; creation of an own advertising report; a new, reasonable donation system; basic revision of the accounting and accounting reports; complete recording of all payment flows; Intensification of fines and penalties; Intensification of credit card obligations for advertising receipts in party newspapers. As I said, The negotiations and discussions with all the political groups are still under way, and I very much hope that we will be able to reach a decision in July.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-15-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00162_d8e29600	Members of Parliament are hereby informed of the matter. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-06-15-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00162_d8e29621	We are discussing at this item on the agenda a request from the SPÖ Group, a proposal for the Party Act. The talks between the government groups and the opposition are currently under way – yes. However, we would also like to know further points, and in view of the constantly emerging scandals with donations, election costs et cetera, this is probably also a real step. The essential points we would like to add: We want to ensure that candidate parties can also be checked so that misuse of tax funds can be avoided in all circumstances. We also want the Court of Auditors and Parliament to have more audit rights when it comes to reviewing the conduct of ministries and government offices. They recall the calamities as regards the award of advisory contracts and awards to agencies. It is really important that the contracts are made public per se and the costs. As far as advisory services are concerned, there is already a recommendation from the Court of Auditors from other reports that internal expertise, i.e. expertise in the ministries that are available, is really being used. [[Unrest in the hall.]] Another point that is very important to us in order to obtain a broad, democratic legitimacy to review is that the President of the Court of Auditors, the President of the Court of Auditors, should be appointed with a two-thirds majority in Parliament. I see a particularly important requirement in the possibility of extending the application for special examinations. At the moment, it is true that only three special tests can run parallel. In the proposal we are making, five would be possible in parallel. I hope that the discussions will be constructive and that we will take these points into account. Thank you.
